-D

Proofs of Theorem
5

We already introduced many notions in
Section

V-C
. We need to introduce a few more in order to write the proof of
Theorem

5
.
In this section, we will consider an equational theory
{E_{\downarrow}}
and an
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction ordering
>
. Given two terms
u,v
, we will denote by
u\geq v
when either
u>v
or
u=_{E_{\downarrow}}v
. In further sections, we will also refer to
{E_{\downarrow}}
as the rewrite set
\{\ell\rightarrow r,r\rightarrow\ell\mid(\ell=r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}\}
when discussing from where a rewrite trace is from.

Rewrite steps

In the proof, we will consider two types of rewrite steps: syntactic and modulo
{E_{\downarrow}}
. We already introduced the notation
t\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}s
and
t\xleftarrow[p,\sigma]{r\ \leftarrow\ \ell}s
. We augment this notation with an equation theory: We write
t\xrightarrow[E,p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}s
when
p\in\mathcal{P}os(t)
,
t_{|p}=_{E}\ell\sigma
and
s=t[r\sigma]_{p}
and
s^{\prime}=_{E_{2}}r\sigma
and
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
. Similarly, we write
s\xleftarrow[p,\sigma,E]{r\ \leftarrow\ \ell}t
when
t\xrightarrow[E,p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}s
. In practice,
E
will either be
{E_{\downarrow}}
or the emptyset
\emptyset
. When
E={E_{\downarrow}}
, it will correspond to the rewrite of
t
by
s
modulo
{E_{\downarrow}}
. When
E=\emptyset
, the equality becomes syntactic and we omit it, hence yielding
t\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}s
.

Recall that compare to standard rewriting, we impose an order between the instantiated terms of the rule,
i.e.
,
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
. Notice that when
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
, we have
t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}s
iff
t\xleftarrow[p,\sigma,{E_{\downarrow}}]{\ell\ \leftarrow\ r}s
.

Rewrite labels and rewrite traces

In Paragraph
V-C4
, we defined the notion on rewrite labels and rewrite traces. As we extended rewrite steps with an equational theory
E
, we also need to update the definition of rewrite labels by just adding the equational theory
E
in the tuple:
A rewrite label is a tuple
\omega=(E,p,\sigma:\,\ell\sim r)
with
{\sim}\in\{\rightarrow,\leftarrow\}
corresponding to the argument of a rewrite step. The

One of the advantages of introducing rewrite traces is that we can define simple operations that will ease the reading of proofs: Given a position
p
and a substitution
\sigma
, we define recursively
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}
,
\mathsf{tr}\sigma
and
\mathsf{tr}^{-1}
as follows.

•

p\cdot\varepsilon=\varepsilon
and
p\cdot(E,q,\alpha:\,\ell\sim r)\mathsf{tr}=(E,p\cdot q,\sigma:\,\ell\sim r)(p\cdot\mathsf{tr})

•

\varepsilon\sigma=\varepsilon
and
((E,q,\alpha:\,\ell\sim r)\mathsf{tr})\sigma=(E,q,\alpha\sigma:\,\ell\sim r)\mathsf{tr}\sigma

•

\varepsilon^{-1}=\varepsilon
and
((E,q,\alpha:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\mathsf{tr})^{-1}=tr^{-1}(E,q,\alpha:\,r\leftarrow\ell)
and
((E,q,\alpha:\,r\leftarrow\ell)\mathsf{tr})^{-1}=tr^{-1}(E,q,\alpha:\,\ell\rightarrow r)

We can provide some basic and useful properties:

Lemma 15
.

Let
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
. For all substitutions
\sigma
, for all term contexts
C[\_]
with
C[\_]_{|p}=\_
, we have

u\sigma\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}\sigma}v\sigma
, and
C[u]\xLeftrightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}}C[v]
, and
v\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{-1}}u
. Moreover, if
u\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
(resp.
u\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
) then
v\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{-1}}u
(resp.
v\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}u
).

Proof.

Directly follows from the definitions.
∎

Ranking function

The transformations presented in
Section

V-C4
that transform peaks into valleys intuitively reduce the altitude of the mountainous landscape, while sometimes increasing the length of the landscape. However, the transformations presented in
Section

V-C5
not only do not decrease the altitude but also increase the length of the landscape, possibly adding terms at the highest altitude. To prove termination of applications of landscape transformations, we rely on an insight found the previous section: we showed that
p
was a strict prefix of all positions of rule applications in
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
. For the rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
, we only had the guarantee that the positions of rule applications were prefixes of
p
(not necessarily strict), but the terms rewritten by the rule were intuitively at a strictly lower altitude.
Therefore, to show termination of our landscape transformations, we will compare both the positions at which a rule is applied, as well as the terms rewritten.

Definition 11
.

We define the
ranking function on rewrite labels
, denoted
\mathsf{rf}
, by
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)=(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
when
\omega
is a right rewrite label
(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
or a left rewrite label
(p,\sigma:\,r\leftarrow\ell)
.

We extend the ranking function to rewrite traces by defining
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr})=\{\!\!\{\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\mid\omega\in\mathsf{tr}\}\!\!\}
, that is the multiset of the ranking values of all rewrite labels in
\mathsf{tr}
.

To compare two instances of the ranking function, we will use the lexicographic order where terms are ordered by
<
the
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction ordering, and where positions are compared through the opposite of the natural prefix order, that is
p_{1}
will be “strictly smaller” than
p_{2}
when
p_{2}
is a strict prefix of
p_{1}
. Intuitively, the closer to
\varepsilon
a position is, the more rewriting is possible on a term. However, this order on positions is not well founded as there is an infinite sequence of “strictly smaller” positions than
p_{1}
. We therefore restrict ourselves to positions contained in a finite set of positions
\mathcal{P}
, that is for
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
to be ordered, they both need to be part of
\mathcal{P}
. As
\mathcal{P}
is finite, the order, that we denote
\prec_{\mathcal{P}}
, becomes well-founded.

The sets of position
\mathcal{P}
will be generated from all the terms in the initial rewrite trace that transformed an equality modulo
E
into a mountainous landscape, as shown in
Section

V-C3
. In particular, if we gather all terms in this initial landscape into a set
\mathcal{S}
, we can show that all terms in any transformed landscape are necessarily smaller (w.r.t.
<
the
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong order) than a term in
\mathcal{S}
. As
<
is well-founded and
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes, the set of terms smaller than terms in
\mathcal{S}
is therefore finite. Hence, we generate
\mathcal{P}
as the set of all positions in all terms smaller than terms in
\mathcal{S}
.

By abuse of notation, given two rewrite traces
\mathsf{tr},\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
, we also denote
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}^{\prime})\prec_{\mathcal{P}}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr})
when using the multiset order induced by the order
\prec_{\mathcal{P}}
on instances of the ranking function on rewrite labels. We show that for any landscape transformation converting a rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}
into a new rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}^{\prime})\prec_{\mathcal{P}}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr})
, ensuring that the application of landscape transformations necessarily terminates.

Remark
.

The termination of applications of landscape transformations does not imply that
generate_rw_th(
)
terminates.

The rewrite labels with equation theory does not impact their value w.r.t. the ranking function, that is
\mathsf{rf}((E,p,\sigma:\,\ell\sim r))=\mathsf{rf}((p,\sigma:\,\ell\sim r))
. Only the inverse operation on rewrite trace preserves the ranking function:

Lemma 16
.

For all rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}^{-1})=\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr})
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathsf{tr}
and by noticing that
\mathsf{rf}((E,q,\alpha:\,\ell\rightarrow r))=\mathsf{rf}((E,q,\alpha:\,r\leftarrow\ell))
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a set of positions. To compare the measurements, we denote by
\prec_{\mathcal{P}}
the strict ordering on rewrite label measurement w.r.t.
\mathcal{P}
, defined as follows:
(p_{1},u_{1},v_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}}(p_{2},u_{2},v_{2})
when
p_{1},p_{2}\in\mathcal{P}
, and either

•

p_{2}
is strict prefix of
p_{1}
, or

•

p_{1}=p_{2}
and either
u_{1}<u_{2}
or (
u_{1}=_{E_{\downarrow}}u_{2}
and
v_{1}<v_{2}
)

We also define equality between measurement as
(p_{1},u_{1},v_{1})\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}}(p_{2},u_{2},v_{2})
when
p_{1}=p_{2}\in\mathcal{P}
,
u_{1}=_{E_{\downarrow}}u_{2}
and
v_{1}=_{E_{\downarrow}}v_{2}
. We naturally define
\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}}
as
{\prec_{\mathcal{P}}}\cup{\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}}}
.

Notice that in the definition of
\prec_{\mathcal{P}}
, the position
p_{1}
is “smaller” than
p_{2}
when
p_{2}
is a strict prefix of
p_{1}
, which is opposite to the natural prefix ordering. Intuitively, the closer to
\varepsilon
a position is, the more rewriting is possible on a term. However, as such ordering is not well-founded, we additionally require the compared positions to be part of the set
\mathcal{P}
. When
\mathcal{P}
is finite, we retrieve the well-foundedness of the ordering, as shown below.

Lemma 17
.

For all finite sets of positions
\mathcal{P}
, the ordering
\prec_{\mathcal{P}}
is well-founded

Proof.

The ordering
\prec_{\mathcal{P}}
is in fact a lexicographic ordering on
(p,u,v)
. To compare the terms
u
and
v
, we use the ordering
<
that is a
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction order, meaning that it is well founded. To compare the position
p
, we use the negation of the prefix ordering, i.e.
p_{1}
is strictly smaller than
p_{2}
when
p_{2}
is a strict prefix of
p_{1}
. This is not well-founded in itself but as we require that both
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are positions in
\mathcal{P}
, which is finite and a parameter of the ordering, we retrieve the well-foundedness of the ordering.
∎

By abuse of notation, given two rewrite traces
\mathsf{tr},\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
, we also denote
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr})\prec_{\mathcal{P}}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}^{\prime})
when using the multiset ordering induced by
\prec_{\mathcal{P}}
on ordering labels.

In additional to the main ranking function
\mathsf{rf}(\cdot)
on rewrite traces, we consider a second ranking function defined on a rewrite traces and the terms it rewrites, that is
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
, denoted
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr},v)
, as the multisets of the terms
\{\!\!\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}\!\!\}
where

u=t_{0}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{1}}t_{1}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{2}}\ldots\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{n}}t_{n}=v

with
\mathsf{tr}=\omega_{1}\ldots\omega_{n}
and the
\omega_{i}
s are rewrite labels. Two instances of this ranking function are compared using the multiset order induced by the
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction order
>
.

Orderly rewriting of terms

The sets of position
\mathcal{P}
used in the ordering above will be generated from all the terms in the initial rewrite trace, by relying on our
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction order
>
(and it’s associated non-strict
\geq
relation). Given a set of terms
\mathcal{M}
, we say that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than a term
t
(or
t
smaller than
\mathcal{M}
) when there exists
s\in\mathcal{M}
such that
s\geq t
. Similarly, we say that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
when for all
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
of
\mathsf{tr}
, if
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}}w
then
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
w
.

Denoting
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})
the set of positions defined as
\{p\in\mathcal{P}os(t)\mid s\in\mathcal{M},s\geq t\}
, we obtain the following property.

Lemma 18
.

If
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes then for all finite set of ground terms
\mathcal{M}
, the set
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})
is finite.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{M}
is ground and finite and since
>
is well founded then the number of
{E_{\downarrow}}
-equivalence classes of ground terms smaller than
\mathcal{M}
is finite. As
{E_{\downarrow}}
having finite equivalence classes implies, we directly conclude.
∎

Saturated sets of rewrite rules

Our generic procedure and its optimisations basically keep merging
{E_{\downarrow}}
-overlapping rewrite rules until a fixpoint is reached, that is until the set of rewrite rules is saturated. We define below the properties satisfied by such saturated set.

Definition 12
.

Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be two sets of rewrites rule. We say that
\mathcal{R}
is
saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
when:

•

\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}

•

for all
(\ell_{1}\rightarrow r_{1}),(\ell_{2}\rightarrow r_{2})\in\mathcal{R}
, for all positions
p
,
(r_{1}\rightarrow\ell_{1}\mathrel{\overset{p}{\rhd\mspace{-4mu}\lhd}}\ell_{2}\rightarrow r_{2})\cup\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}

•

for all
(\ell_{1}\rightarrow r_{1})\in\mathcal{R}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
, for all
(\ell_{2}\rightarrow r_{2})\in\mathcal{R}
, for all positions
p
,
(\ell_{1}\rightarrow r_{1}\mathrel{\overset{p}{\rhd}}\ell_{2}\rightarrow r_{2})\cup\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}

•

for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
,
\{r\rightarrow\ell\}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}

-D
1
Normalisation rules

Recall that
>
is a
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction order compatible with
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Before we state stating the correctness of our normalisation rules, we show that if
u>v
then
\mathit{vars}(v)\subseteq\mathit{vars}(u)
. Indeed, assume that
u
contains a variable
x
that is not a variable of
v
. Thus
v>u=C[x,\ldots,x]
. As
>
is stable by application of substitutions, we deduce that
v>C[v,\ldots,v]>C[C[v,\ldots,v],\ldots,C[v,\ldots,v]]>\ldots
leading to a contradiction with
>
being well founded.

Lemma 19

(Rule
NormR
)
.

Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
be a ground rewrite step and
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
. Let
\mathcal{M}
a set of terms greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.
If
r=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\xrightarrow[p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}s
and
\ell^{\prime}>r^{\prime}
then there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that:

u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ s}u[s\sigma]_{p}\xleftarrow[p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]{r^{\prime}\ \leftarrow\ \ell^{\prime}}u[t\sigma]_{p}\xLeftarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma}v

and denoting
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow s)(p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma:\,r^{\prime}\leftarrow\ell^{\prime})(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma)
, we deduce that
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
is a ground syntactic rewrite trace and:

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
or (
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
and
\omega\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma
).

Proof.

By definition,
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
implies
v=u[r\sigma]_{p}
, and
u_{|p}=\ell\sigma
and
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
.
By definition
t\xrightarrow[p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}s
, we deduce that
t_{|p^{\prime}}=\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and
s=t[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{p^{\prime}}
. Since
r=_{E_{\downarrow}}t
, there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
a syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
t\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}r
. Thus,
t\sigma\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma}r\sigma
, implying
u[t\sigma]_{p}\xLeftarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma}v
. Since
s=t[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{p^{\prime}}
and
t_{|p^{\prime}}=\ell\sigma^{\prime}
, we have
u[s\sigma]_{p}\xleftarrow[p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]{r^{\prime}\ \leftarrow\ \ell^{\prime}}u[t\sigma]_{p}
. Recall that
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
meaning that
\mathit{vars}(r^{\prime})\subseteq\mathit{vars}(\ell^{\prime})
.

Recall that
\ell^{\prime}>r^{\prime}
. Hence,
t=t[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{p^{\prime}}>t[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{p^{\prime}}=s
. As
>
is
{E_{\downarrow}}
-compatible and
r=_{E_{\downarrow}}t
, we deduce
r>s
. As
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
, we deduce that
\ell\sigma>s\sigma
. Thus,
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ s}u[s\sigma]_{p}
and so
Item

P1
holds. By construction of
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
, as
\mathcal{M}
is greater then
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we directly obtain that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, i.e.
Item

P2
.

Finally, we already showed that
r\sigma>s\sigma
thus
(p,s\sigma,\ell\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. Additionally, if
p^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon
then we trivially have that
(p\cdot p^{\prime},r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma,\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. Otherwise,
r=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and
s=r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. Hence
r\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
and so
(p,r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma,\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. It remains to consider
\omega\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma
, that is
\omega=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha\sigma:\,b\leftarrow a)
with
p\leq p^{\prime\prime}
: Once again, if
p^{\prime\prime}\neq p
then we directly have that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. Otherwise, it implies that
a\alpha\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
. Therefore since
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
, we deduce that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
which concludes the proof of
Item

P3
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
when
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
u[s\sigma]
u[t\sigma]_{p}
v
\ell\rightarrow s
p,\sigma
r^{\prime}\leftarrow\ell^{\prime}
p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma

when
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
u[s\sigma]
u[t\sigma]_{p}
v
\ell\rightarrow s
p,\sigma
r^{\prime}\leftarrow\ell^{\prime}
p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma

∎

Lemma

19
focuses only a right rewrite step
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
but we can easily have similar result on a left rewrite step by using the properties of the inverse operator in
Lemmas

16
and
15
.

Lemma 20

(Rule
NormL
)
.

Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
be a ground rewrite step and
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
. Let
\mathcal{M}
a set of terms greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.
If
\ell=f(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n})
and
\ell_{i}=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\xrightarrow[p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}s
and
\ell^{\prime}>r^{\prime}
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\{r\rightarrow\ell[s]_{i},\ell[s]_{i}\rightarrow r,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
such that:

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
.

Proof.

Let us denote
w=\ell[s]_{i}
. Since
\ell_{|i}=_{E_{\downarrow}}t
, there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
a syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
\ell_{|i}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{t}}t
. By definition of
t\xrightarrow[p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}s
, we have
t_{|p^{\prime}}=\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and
s=t[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{p^{\prime}}
.
Moreover,
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
implies
u_{|p}=\ell\sigma
and
v=u[r\sigma]_{p}
. Therefore,
u\xRightarrow{p\cdot i\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma}u[\ell\sigma[t\sigma]_{i}]_{p}\xrightarrow[p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}u[\ell\sigma[t\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]_{p^{\prime}}]_{i}]_{p}=u[\ell\sigma[s\sigma]_{i}]_{p}=u[w\sigma]_{p}
.

Let us define
\omega_{M}=(p,\sigma:\,w\rightarrow r)
when
w\sigma>r\sigma
and
\omega_{M}=(p,\sigma:\,w\leftarrow r)
otherwise. In both cases, we deduce that
u[w\sigma]_{p}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{M}}u[r\sigma]_{p}=v
. Defining
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=(p\cdot i\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma)(p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\omega_{M}
, we deduce that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
proving
Item

P1
.
Since
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
thus proving
Item

P2
.

We now focus on
Item

P3
: Since
\ell^{\prime}>r^{\prime}
, we deduce that
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and so
\ell[t]_{i}>\ell[s]_{i}
. As
>
is
{E_{\downarrow}}
-compatible and
t=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell_{i}
, we deduce that
\ell>w
, implying
\ell\sigma>w\sigma
.
If
w\sigma>r\sigma
then we obtain
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})=(p,r\sigma,w\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
else if
r\sigma>w\sigma
then
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})=(p,w\sigma,r\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
, else
r\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}w\sigma
but
\ell\sigma>w\sigma
hence
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})=(p,w\sigma,r\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
.

Consider now
\omega=(p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
, we have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)=(p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma,\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma)
. As
p
is a strict prefix of
p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime}
, we directly have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. For the same reason, we deduce that for all
\omega\in p\cdot i\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)<(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:

when
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
and
w\sigma<r\sigma

\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
u[\ell\sigma[t\sigma]_{i}]_{p}
u[w\sigma]_{p}
v
p\cdot i\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
w\leftarrow r
p,\sigma

when
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
and
w\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma

\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
u[\ell\sigma[t\sigma]_{i}]_{p}
u[w\sigma]_{p}
v
p\cdot i\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
w\leftarrow r
p,\sigma

when
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
and
w\sigma>r\sigma

\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
u[\ell\sigma[t\sigma]_{i}]_{p}
u[w\sigma]_{p}
v
p\cdot i\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
w\rightarrow r
p,\sigma

when
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma

\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
u[\ell\sigma[t\sigma]_{i}]_{p}
u[w\sigma]_{p}
v
p\cdot i\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
p\cdot i\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
w\leftarrow r
p,\sigma

∎

Lemma 21

(Rule
Sub
)
.

Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
be a ground rewrite step and
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
. Let
\mathcal{M}
a set of terms greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.
If there exist
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
, a term context
C[\_]
and a substitution
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}\ell
and
C[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
then there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
such that:

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
.

Proof.

By definition of
C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}\ell
, there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
a syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
\ell\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]
and for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega)\neq\varepsilon
. As
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
implies
u_{|p}=\ell\sigma
and
v=u[r\sigma]_{p}
, we deduce that
u\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma}u[C\sigma[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]_{p}
.
Similarly,
C[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
implies that there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
a syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
C[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}r
. Hence
u[C\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]_{p}\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma}v
.

Recall that
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
implies
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
. By contradiction, assume that
r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma>\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
. Since
>
is a
{E_{\downarrow}}
-compatible reduction ordering, we deduce that
r\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}C\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]>C\sigma[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\sigma
and so
r\sigma>\ell\sigma
, which is a contradiction with
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
. Therefore
r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma\leq\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
and so we obtain
C\sigma[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]\xrightarrow[p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}C\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]
with
C[\_]_{|p^{\prime}}=\_
which entails
u[C\sigma[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]_{p}\xrightarrow[p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}u[C\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]_{p}
. Defining
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma)(p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma)
, we deduce that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, yielding
Item

P1
.
As
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we also directly obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, hence proving
Item

P2
.

We now focus on
Item

P3
. We know that for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega)\neq\varepsilon
. Hence for all
\omega\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma
,
p,\mathit{pos}(\omega)
meaning that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. Let us denote
\omega^{\prime}=(p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
. Recall that
C[\_]_{|p^{\prime}}=\_
. Thus, if
p^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon
then we also directly have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega^{\prime})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
; and otherwise
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\sigma
and
r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
which implies
\mathsf{rf}(\omega^{\prime})=(p,r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma,\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma)\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. Therefore, in both cases, we have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega^{\prime})\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
.
Finally, for all
(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,s\rightarrow t)\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma
, if
p^{\prime\prime}=p
then
s\alpha=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}t\alpha=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r\sigma
. As
\ell\sigma\geq r\sigma
, we deduce that either
(p^{\prime\prime},t\alpha,s\alpha)\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. We conclude the proof of
Item

P3
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
u[C\sigma[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]_{p}
u[C\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]_{p}
v
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma

∎

Lemma 22

(Rule
Eq
)
.

Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
be a ground rewrite step and
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
. Let
\mathcal{M}
a set of terms greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.
If
\ell=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
then there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that:

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
.

Proof.

By definition of
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
,
u_{|p}=\ell\sigma
and
v_{|p}=r\sigma
. As
\ell=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
, there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{M}
a syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
\ell\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}}r
. Thus,
u_{|p}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}\sigma}v_{|p}
and so
u\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}\sigma}v
. Defining
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}\sigma
, we directly conclude
Item

P1
. As
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we also deduce by construction of
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, hence proving
Item

P2
. Moreover, as
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma=u_{|p}
, we deduce that for all
\omega=(p^{\prime},\alpha:\,s\rightarrow t)\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
,
p\leq p^{\prime}
and if
p=p^{\prime}
then
s\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
. Hence we deduce that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
which allows us to conclude the proof of
Item

P3
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
u
v
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}\sigma

∎

Lemma 23
.

Let
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
and
\mathcal{R}
two sets of rewrite rules such that
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
. Assume that
\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
be a ground rewrite step. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms greater than
u
and
v
. Let
q
be a position and
a,b
two terms. There exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that:

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v

P3

if
(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
then for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
, we have in fact
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}_{0}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\ldots\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\mathcal{R}_{n}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
for some
\mathcal{R}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{R}_{n}
. We show the following result:

For all
i\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
, for all
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
(resp.
u\xleftarrow[p,\sigma]{r\ \leftarrow\ \ell}v
), if
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}_{i}
and
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u,v
and
(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
then there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that:

•

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v

•

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v

•

for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)

We prove this result by induction on
((p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma),n-i)
using the lexicographic ordering on pair, using
\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}
to compare the first element of the pair and standard natural number ordering on the second element of the pair.

Once we proved that the result holds for
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
, we can derive the result for
u\xleftarrow[p,\sigma]{r\ \leftarrow\ \ell}v
by applying the result on
v\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}u
, leading to the existence of a syntactic trace
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that
v\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}u
,
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into
v\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}u
and for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
. We conclude by taking
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=\mathsf{tr}_{0}^{-1}
and by applying
Lemmas

15
and
16
.

We now focus on proving the result for
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}v
.

Base case
((p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma),0)
:

In such a case, we have
i=n
and so
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
. Hence the result directly holds with
\mathsf{tr}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
.

Inductive step
((p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma),n-i)
:

Let us look at the normalisation rule
\mathcal{R}_{i}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
. If
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
then we can apply our inductive hypothesis on
((p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma),n-i-1)
which allows us to conclude. Therefore we assume that
\ell\rightarrow r\not\in\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
. We do a case analysis on the normalisation rule
\mathcal{R}_{i}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
.

•

Rule
NormR
:
In such a case,
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow r\}
,
r=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}}s
and
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow s\}
. As
r=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}}s
, we have
r=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\xrightarrow[p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}s
for some
p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
and
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Hence we can apply
Lemma

19
to obtain that there exist a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow s)(p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma:\,r^{\prime}\leftarrow\ell^{\prime})(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma)
such that

u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ s}u[s\sigma]_{p}\xleftarrow[p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]{r^{\prime}\ \leftarrow\ \ell^{\prime}}u[t\sigma]_{p}\xLeftarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma}v

and the following properties hold:

–

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

–

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

–

for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
or (
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
and
\omega\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma
)

From the last property, we deduce that
(p,s\sigma,\ell\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
and
(p\cdot p^{\prime},r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma,\ell\sigma^{\prime}\sigma)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. Hence, as
(\ell\rightarrow s)\in\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
, we can apply our inductive hypothesis on
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ s}u[s\sigma]_{p}
which gives us the existence of a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{2}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{2}}u[s\sigma]_{p}
, which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
, and for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{2}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
.

Similarly, as
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{0}
, we can apply our inductive hypothesis on
u[s\sigma]_{p}\xleftarrow[p\cdot p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]{r^{\prime}\ \leftarrow\ \ell^{\prime}}u[t\sigma]_{p}
which implies the existence of a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{3}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that
u[s\sigma]_{p}\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{3}}u[t\sigma]_{p}
, which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
, and for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{3}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
. We conclude by taking
\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}_{2}\mathsf{tr}_{3}(p\cdot p^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}\sigma)
.

•

Rule
NormL
:
In such a case,
\ell=f(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})
,
\ell_{j}=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}}\ell^{\prime}_{j}
,
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow r\}
and
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\cup\{\ell[\ell^{\prime}_{j}]_{j}\rightarrow r,r\rightarrow\ell[\ell^{\prime}_{j}]_{j}\}
. Denoting
s=\ell[\ell^{\prime}_{j}]_{j}
and applying
Lemma

20
, we deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=\omega_{1}\ldots\omega_{m}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\{r\rightarrow s,s\rightarrow r,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
such that:

u=u_{0}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{1}}u_{1}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{2}}\ldots\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{m}}u_{m}=v

and
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
derives, and for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{j})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
.

Take
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
such that
\omega_{j}=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,w\sim w^{\prime})
. If
(w=w^{\prime})\in{E_{\downarrow}}
then we define
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}=\omega_{j}
else if
(w\sim w^{\prime})\in\{s\leftarrow r,s\rightarrow r\}
then
(w\sim w^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
else we have
(w\sim w^{\prime})=(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{0}
. In the last two cases, since
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{j})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
we can apply our inductive hypothesis on
u_{j-1}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{j}}u_{j}
which implies the existence of a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that
u_{j-1}\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}}u_{j}
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
and for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
. We conclude by taking
\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{m}

•

Rule
Eq
:
In such a case
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow r\}
and
\ell=_{E_{\downarrow}}r
. Applying
Lemma

22
, the result directly holds.

•

Rule
Sub
:
In such a case
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow r\}
and there exist
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
, a term context
C[\_]
and a substitution
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}\ell
and
C[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
. Applying
Lemma

21
, we deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=\omega_{1}\ldots\omega_{m}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
such that:

u=u_{0}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{1}}u_{1}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{2}}\ldots\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{m}}u_{m}=v

and
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
, and for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{j})\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
. Thus
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{j})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.

Take
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
such that
\omega_{j}=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,w\sim t)
. If
(w=t)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
then we define
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}=\omega_{j}
else
(w\sim t)=(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
. As
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{j})\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)
, we deduce that
(\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{j}),n-i-1)
is strictly smaller than
((p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma),n-i)
.
We can therefore apply our inductive hypothesis on
u_{j-1}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{j}}u_{j}
which implies the existence of a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that
u_{j-1}\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}}u_{j}
which
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into, and for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{j}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(q,a,b)
. We conclude by taking
\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{m}
.∎

Corollary 2
.

Let
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
and
\mathcal{R}
two sets of rewrite rules such that
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
. Assume that
\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0},\mathsf{tr}_{1}
be two ground syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms. Let
u,v
two ground terms such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, and
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into both
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
and
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
.

There exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{2}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{2}}v
which
\mathcal{M}
orderly rewrites into, and if for all
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
, there exists
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
then
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{2})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
.

Proof.

Direct application of
Lemma

23
on each
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}_{1}
where
(q,a,b)
is taken as
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
where
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
and
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
(its existence is given as hypothesis).
∎

We complete this section by proving some useful properties on the normalisation rules. Given two sets of rewrite rules
\mathcal{R}_{1},\mathcal{R}_{2}
, we will say that
\mathcal{R}_{1}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{2}
w.r.t
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
if for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}_{2}
, either
\ell=_{E_{\downarrow}}r
or there exists
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{1}
, a term context
C[\_]
and a substitution such that
C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma]=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}\ell
and
C[r^{\prime}\sigma]=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
and
r^{\prime}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
and
\forall p>\varepsilon.\ell^{\prime}_{|p}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
.

Lemma 24
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{R}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules.

1.

\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
implies
\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}_{0}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{R}_{0}

2.

\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{R}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{0}
w.r.t
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
implies
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{0}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}

3.

If
\mathcal{R}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{0}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
then
\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}_{0}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}

4.

\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
implies there exist
\mathcal{R}_{in},\mathcal{R}_{rm}
such that
\{\ell\rightarrow r\}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{in}\cup\mathcal{R}_{rm}
, and
\mathcal{R}_{in}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{R}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{rm}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}

Proof.

Item

1
is direct by definition of the normalisation rules in
Figure

1
. If
\mathcal{R}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{0}
then by definition for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}_{0}
, either
\ell=_{E_{\downarrow}}r
or there exists
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
, a term context
C[\_]
and a substitution such that
C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma]=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}\ell
and
C[r^{\prime}\sigma]=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
and
r^{\prime}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
and
\forall p>\varepsilon.\ell^{\prime}_{|p}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
. Hence, we can successively apply the rules
Sub
and
Eq
to obtain that
\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}_{0}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}
, yielding
Item

3
.

We now look at
Item

2
. We prove the result by induction on the length of the derivation
\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
.

Base case
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
:

The result trivially holds.

Inductive step
\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
:

By inductive hypothesis, we know that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{0}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. By definition,
\mathcal{R}_{0}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{0}
and for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}_{0}
, either
\ell=_{E_{\downarrow}}r
or there exists
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}
, a term context
C[\_]
and a substitution such that
C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma]=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}\ell
and
C[r^{\prime}\sigma]=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r
and
r^{\prime}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
and
\forall p>\varepsilon.\ell^{\prime}_{|p}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
. Hence, we only need to look at the case where
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\not\in\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
(in the other cases, the result would hold). We do a case analysis on the normalisation rule applied.

•

Rule
NormL
:
Cannot be the case as
\forall p>\varepsilon.\ell^{\prime}_{|p}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}

•

Rule
NormR
:
Cannot be the case as
r^{\prime}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}

•

Rule
Sub
:
In such a case, there exist
(\ell^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
, a term context
C^{\prime}[\_]
and a substitution
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
C^{\prime}[r^{\prime\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}r^{\prime}
and
C^{\prime}[\ell^{\prime\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}\ell^{\prime}
and
r^{\prime\prime}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
and
\forall p>\varepsilon.\ell^{\prime\prime}_{|p}\not\rightarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}/{E_{\downarrow}}}
. Hence
\ell=^{s}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}C[C^{\prime}\sigma[\ell^{\prime\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]
and
r=_{{E_{\downarrow}}}C[C^{\prime}\sigma[r^{\prime\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma]]
. Defining
C^{\prime\prime}[\_]=C[C^{\prime}\sigma[\_]]
and
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}\sigma
, the result holds.

•

Rule
Eq
:
In such a case,
\ell^{\prime}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r^{\prime}
. Hence
C[\ell^{\prime}\sigma]=_{E_{\downarrow}}C[r^{\prime}\sigma]
which implies
\ell=_{E_{\downarrow}}r
.

This concludes the proof of
Item

2
.

We finally focus on
Item

4
. Once again, we prove the result by induction on the length of the derivation
\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
.

Base case
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
:

The result trivially holds by taking
\mathcal{R}_{in}=\{\ell\rightarrow r\}
and
\mathcal{R}_{rm}=\emptyset
.

Inductive step
\mathcal{R}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
:

By inductive hypothesis, we know that for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
, there exist
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{in},\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{rm}
such that
\{\ell\rightarrow r\}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{in}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{rm}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{in}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{rm}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. If the rule applied in
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
does not affect rules in
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{in}
then it would imply that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{in}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and by
Item

2
, we know that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{rm}
, which allows us to conclude. If the rule applied in
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
affect a rule in
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{in}
, say
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
, i.e.
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{in}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{in}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
we can do a simple case analysis:

•

Rule
NormL
or
NormR
:
In such a case,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{add}
for some
\mathcal{R}_{add}
. We conclude by defining
\mathcal{R}_{rm}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{rm}
and
\mathcal{R}_{in}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{in}\cup\mathcal{R}_{add}
and applying
Item

2
.

•

Rule
Sub
or
Eq
:
In such a case
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
. We conclude by defining
\mathcal{R}_{rm}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{rm}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}
and
\mathcal{R}_{in}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{in}
and applying
Item

2
.∎

Lemma 25
.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{R}_{n}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n+1}
and
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}\cup\mathcal{R}_{i}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
. Then for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{R}_{j}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.

Proof.

We prove the result by induction on
i
.

Base case
i=0
:

In such a case,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{R}_{j}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}
. Hence we trivially have that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}
.

Inductive step
i>0
:

By inductive hypothesis,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i-1}\mathcal{R}_{j}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}
. Moreover, by hypothesis,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}\cup\mathcal{R}_{i}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.
Therefore, by
Lemma

24
, for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{i-1}\mathcal{R}_{j}
, there exist
\mathcal{R}_{rm},\mathcal{R}_{in}
such that
\{\ell\rightarrow r\}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{rm}\cup\mathcal{R}_{in}
such that
\mathcal{R}_{in}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{rm}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
.
As
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}\cup\mathcal{R}_{i}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
, then, denoting the rules in
\mathcal{R}_{in}
by
\{\ell_{1}\rightarrow r_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m}\rightarrow r_{m}\}
, we deduce that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{rm}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
and there exist
\mathcal{R}^{1}_{rm},\mathcal{R}^{1}_{in},\ldots,\mathcal{R}^{m}_{rm},\mathcal{R}^{m}_{in}
such that for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
\{\ell_{j}\rightarrow r_{j}\}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{j}_{rm}\cup\mathcal{R}^{j}_{in}
,
\mathcal{R}^{j}_{in}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
and
\mathcal{R}^{j}_{rm}
is subsumed by
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
. Hence,

\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow r\}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{rm}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{R}^{j}_{rm}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}

Thus, we deduce that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i-1}\mathcal{R}_{j}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
. Finally, as
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i}\cup\mathcal{R}_{i}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
, we deduce once again by
Lemma

24
that for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}_{i}
, there exist
\mathcal{R}_{rm},\mathcal{R}_{in}
such that
\{\ell\rightarrow r\}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{rm}\cup\mathcal{R}_{in}
such that
\mathcal{R}_{in}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
subsumes
\mathcal{R}_{rm}
w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Therefore, we deduce that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow r\}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
and so
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{R}_{j}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.
∎

-D
2
Overlapping transformations

In this section, we look at the cases of two successive rules that overlap.

Lemma 26
.

Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Let
u\xleftarrow[p,\sigma]{r\ \leftarrow\ \ell}t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some ground rewrite steps. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,r\leftarrow\ell)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
with
\{\ell\rightarrow r,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms that rewrites into
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p^{\prime}=p\cdot q
, and
q\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(\ell)
, and
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
, and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
\mathcal{R}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that:

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

Proof.

By definition, there exists a term context
C[\_]
such that
t=C[\ell\sigma]
,
u=C[r\sigma]
,
C[\_]_{|p}=\_
,
\ell_{|q}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
,
v=C[r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]
. Since
\ell_{|q}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, we can denote
\gamma=\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}
to obtain
\ell_{|q}\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\gamma
. Hence
\ell_{|q}
and
\ell^{\prime}
are
{E_{\downarrow}}
-unifiable.
Let
\theta\in\mathrm{mgu}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}(\ell_{|q},\ell^{\prime})
. There exists
\sigma_{3}
such that
\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}(\theta\sigma_{3})_{\mathit{dom}(\gamma)}
. Moreover, we also know that there exists
\ell_{3}\rightarrow r_{3}\in(r\rightarrow\ell\mathrel{\overset{q}{\rhd}}\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
such that
\ell_{3}=r\theta
,
r_{3}=\ell\theta[r^{\prime}\theta]_{q}
. Hence
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\gamma=r\sigma
. We also have
r_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\gamma[r^{\prime}\gamma]_{q}=\ell\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}
.

As
\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}(\theta\sigma_{3})_{\mathit{dom}(\gamma)}
, we deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
r\sigma\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}
. Similarly, there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
r_{3}\sigma_{3}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}\ell\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}
. We therefore obtain the following sequence:

u=C[r\sigma]\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}}C[\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}]\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{3}}C[r_{3}\sigma_{3}]\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}}C[\ell\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]=v

with
\omega_{3}=(p,\sigma_{3}:\,\ell_{3}\rightarrow r_{3})
if
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}\geq r_{3}\sigma_{3}
and
\omega_{3}=(p,\sigma_{3}:\,\ell_{3}\leftarrow r_{3})
otherwise.
By defining
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}=(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L})\omega_{3}(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R})
, we obtain that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
. Moreover, as
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
.

We now order the labels of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
w.r.t.
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
. Let us denote
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,r\leftarrow\ell)
and
\omega^{\prime}=({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
.
Since
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and
\ell_{|q}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, we deduce that
\ell\sigma>r_{3}\sigma_{3}
. We do a case analysis on the comparison between
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}
and
r_{3}\sigma_{3}
.

•

Case
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}\geq r_{3}\sigma_{3}
: Since
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
, we deduce that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{3})=(p,r_{3}\sigma_{3},\ell_{3}\sigma_{3})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)=\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Moreover, for all
\omega_{1}=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,a\rightarrow b)\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
p\leq p^{\prime\prime}
and if
p=p^{\prime\prime}
then
a\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
. Since
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
, we deduce that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)=\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Finally, for all
\omega_{1}=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,a\rightarrow b)\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
p\leq p^{\prime\prime}
and if
p=p^{\prime\prime}
then
a\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}r_{3}\sigma_{3}
. Recall that

thus when

then

. We deduce that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)=\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. We therefore conclude that for all
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
, there exist
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
.

and

, we deduce that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{3})=(p,\ell_{3}\sigma_{3},r_{3}\sigma_{3})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)=\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Moreover, for all

,
p\leq p^{\prime\prime}
and if
p=p^{\prime\prime}
then
a\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
. Since
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
, we deduce that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)=\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Finally, for all
\omega_{1}=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,a\rightarrow b)\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
p\leq p^{\prime\prime}
and if
p=p^{\prime\prime}
then
a\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}r_{3}\sigma_{3}
and
q=\varepsilon
. In such a case (
p=p^{\prime\prime}
),
r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r_{3}\sigma_{3}
and
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. Thus,
a\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}<\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and so
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega^{\prime})
. We therefore conclude that for all
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
, there exist
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that

.

, there exist
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
.

Notice that, denoting
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\cup(r\rightarrow\ell\mathrel{\overset{q}{\rhd\mspace{-4mu}\lhd}}\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))

to deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace

, which is smaller than

. This conclude the proof.
