Lemma 27
.

Assume that
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes. Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some ground be rewrite steps. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
with
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
and
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p^{\prime}=p\cdot q
, and
q\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(r)
, and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

Proof.

By definition, there exists a term context
C[\_]
such that
t=C[r\sigma]
,
u=C[\ell\sigma]
,
C[\_]_{|p}=\_
,
r_{|q}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
,
v=C[\ell\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]
. Since
r_{|q}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, we can denote
\gamma=\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}
to obtain
r_{|q}\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\gamma
. Hence
r_{|q}
and
\ell^{\prime}
are
{E_{\downarrow}}
-unifiable.
Let
\theta\in\mathrm{mgu}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}(r_{|q},\ell^{\prime})
. There exists
\sigma_{3}
such that
\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}(\theta\sigma_{3})_{\mathit{dom}(\gamma)}
. Moreover, we also know that there exists
\ell_{3}\rightarrow r_{3}\in(\ell\rightarrow r\mathrel{\overset{q}{\rhd}}\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
such that
\ell_{3}=\ell\theta
,
r_{3}=r\theta[r^{\prime}\theta]_{q}
. Hence
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\gamma=\ell\sigma
. We also have
r_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\gamma[r^{\prime}\gamma]_{q}=r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}
.

As
\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}(\theta\sigma_{3})_{\mathit{dom}(\gamma)}
, we deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
\ell\sigma\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}
. Since
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
and
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes, we deduce that
\ell
and
r
are not variables. Therefore, for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega)\neq\varepsilon
.
Similarly, there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
r_{3}\sigma_{3}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}
and if
q\neq\varepsilon
then for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega)\neq\varepsilon
. As
>
is a
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction ordering and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma"
, we deduce
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma>r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r_{3}\sigma_{3}
.
We therefore obtain the following sequence:

u=C[\ell\sigma]\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}}C[\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}]\xRightarrow{\omega_{3}}C[r_{3}\sigma_{3}]\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}}C[r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]=v

with
\omega_{3}=(p,\sigma_{3}:\,\ell_{3}\rightarrow r_{3})
.
By defining
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}=(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L})\omega_{3}(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R})
, we obtain that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
. Moreover, as
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
that
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
.

We now order the labels of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
w.r.t.
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
. Let us denote
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
and
\omega^{\prime}=({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
.
We already proved that
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma>r_{3}\sigma_{3}
. Thus
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{3})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
.
Moreover, we already proved that for all
\omega_{1}
in
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})\neq\varepsilon
. Hence for all
\omega_{1}
in
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
p<\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})
, implying that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Finally, if
q\neq\varepsilon
then we also showed that for all
\omega_{1}
in
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})\neq\varepsilon
, and so for all
\omega_{1}
in
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
p<\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})
, implying that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. We now focus on
q=\varepsilon
. In such a case,
p=p^{\prime}
and
for all
\omega_{1}=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,a\rightarrow b)\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}
, if
p^{\prime\prime}=p
then
a\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
else
p<p^{\prime\prime}
. Hence
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})=(p^{\prime\prime},b\alpha,a\alpha)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p^{\prime},r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime})=\mathsf{rf}(\omega^{\prime})
.
We therefore conclude that for all
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
, there exist
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
t
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u
C[\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}]
C[r_{3}\sigma_{3}]
v
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}\rightarrow r_{3}\sigma_{3}
p,\sigma_{3}
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}

Notice that, denoting
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\cup(\ell\rightarrow r\mathrel{\overset{q}{\rhd}}\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))
, we have that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
is a syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Since
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
is from
\mathcal{R}
, it is naturally also from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Note that by
Definition

12
, we know that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}
and
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
. Thus, we can apply
Corollary

2
to deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
\mathcal{R}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
which
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into, and
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
. This conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 28
.

Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some ground rewrite steps. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
with
(\ell\rightarrow r),(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p^{\prime}=p\cdot q
, and
q\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(r)
, and
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
, and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and (
\ell\in\mathcal{X}
implies
\ell\in\mathit{vars}(r)
) then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

Proof.

By definition, there exists a term context
C[\_]
such that
t=C[r\sigma]
,
u=C[\ell\sigma]
,
C[\_]_{|p}=\_
,
r_{|q}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
,
v=C[\ell\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]
. Since
r_{|q}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, we can denote
\gamma=\sigma\cup\sigma^{\prime}
to obtain
r_{|q}\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\gamma
. Hence
r_{|q}
and
\ell^{\prime}
are
{E_{\downarrow}}
-unifiable.
Let
\theta\in\mathrm{mgu}_{{E_{\downarrow}}}(r_{|q},\ell^{\prime})
. There exists
\sigma_{3}
such that
\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}(\theta\sigma_{3})_{\mathit{dom}(\gamma)}
. Moreover, we also know that there exists
\ell_{3}\rightarrow r_{3}\in(\ell\rightarrow r\mathrel{\overset{q}{\rhd}}\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
such that
\ell_{3}=\ell\theta
,
r_{3}=r\theta[r^{\prime}\theta]_{q}
. Hence
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\gamma=\ell\sigma
. We also have
r_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\gamma[r^{\prime}\gamma]_{q}=r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}
.

As
\gamma=_{E_{\downarrow}}(\theta\sigma_{3})_{\mathit{dom}(\gamma)}
, we deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
\ell\sigma\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}
.
By hypothesis, we know that
\ell\in\mathcal{X}
implies
\ell\in\mathit{vars}(r)
. However,
>
is a reduction ordering, hence if
a
is a strict subterm of
b
then
a\not>b
(otherwise we would have
b=C^{\prime}[a]
and so
a>C^{\prime}[a]>C^{\prime}[C^{\prime}[a]]>\ldots
). Note that if
a=b
then we also have
a\not>b
. Thus, as
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
, we deduce that
\ell\not\in\mathcal{X}
, meaning that for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega)\neq\varepsilon
.

Similarly, there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
r_{3}\sigma_{3}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}
and if
q\neq\varepsilon
then for all
\omega\in\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega)\neq\varepsilon
. As
>
is a
{E_{\downarrow}}
-strong reduction ordering and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma"
, we deduce
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\sigma>r\sigma>r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r_{3}\sigma_{3}
.
We therefore obtain the following sequence:

u=C[\ell\sigma]\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}}C[\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}]\xRightarrow{\omega_{3}}C[r_{3}\sigma_{3}]\xRightarrow{p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}}C[r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]=v

with
\omega_{3}=(p,\sigma_{3}:\,\ell_{3}\rightarrow r_{3})
.
By defining
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}=(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L})\omega_{3}(p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R})
, we obtain that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
. Moreover, as
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
.

We now order the labels of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
w.r.t.
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
. Let us denote
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
and
\omega^{\prime}=({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
.
We already proved that
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\sigma>r\sigma>r_{3}\sigma_{3}
. Thus
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{3})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
.
Moreover, we already proved that for all
\omega_{1}
in
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})\neq\varepsilon
. Hence for all
\omega_{1}
in
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
p<\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})
, implying that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Finally, if
q\neq\varepsilon
then we also showed that for all
\omega_{1}
in
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})\neq\varepsilon
, and so for all
\omega_{1}
in
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}
,
p<\mathit{pos}(\omega_{1})
, implying that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. We now focus on
q=\varepsilon
. In such a case,
p=p^{\prime}
and
for all
\omega_{1}=(p^{\prime\prime},\alpha:\,a\rightarrow b)\in p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}
, if
p^{\prime\prime}=p
then
a\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}b\alpha=_{E_{\downarrow}}r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
else
p<p^{\prime\prime}
. Hence
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})=(p^{\prime\prime},b\alpha,a\alpha)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p^{\prime},r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime})=\mathsf{rf}(\omega^{\prime})
.
We therefore conclude that for all
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
, there exist
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
t
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u
C[\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}]
C[r_{3}\sigma_{3}]
v
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{L}
\ell_{3}\sigma_{3}\rightarrow r_{3}\sigma_{3}
p,\sigma_{3}
p\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{R}

Notice that, denoting
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\cup(\ell\rightarrow r\mathrel{\overset{q}{\rhd}}\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))
, we have that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
is a syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Since
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
is from
\mathcal{R}
, it is naturally also from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Note that by
Definition

12
, we know that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}
and
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
. Thus, we can apply
Corollary

2
to deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
\mathcal{R}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
, and
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
. This conclude the proof.
∎

-D
3
Non-overlapping transformations

In the previous section, we look at the cases of two successive non-overlapping rules.

Lemma 29
.

Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Let
u\xleftarrow[p,\sigma]{r\ \leftarrow\ \ell}t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some ground rewrite steps. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,r\leftarrow\ell)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
with
(\ell\rightarrow r),(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p^{\prime}=p\cdot q
, and
q\not\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(\ell)
, and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

Proof.

By definition, we have there exist a term context
C[\_]
, two positions
q_{x},q^{\prime}
and a variable
x
such that
u=C[r\sigma]
,
C[\_]_{|p}=\_
,
t=C[\ell\sigma]
,
q=q_{x}\cdot q^{\prime}
,
\ell_{|q_{x}}=x\in\mathcal{X}
,
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and
C[\ell\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]=v
.
Since
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=_{E_{\downarrow}}x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}
, there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. Hence, we can define
\mathsf{tr}_{M}=\mathsf{tr}_{L}(\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
to obtain
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}}r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=v_{|p^{\prime}}
. From
Lemma

15
, we deduce that
v_{|p^{\prime}}\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}
.

Let
\theta
be the substitution such that
y\theta=y\sigma
for all
y\in\mathit{vars}(r)\setminus\{x\}
and
x\theta=x\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q^{\prime}}
.
If
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
are the positions of
x
in
r
, thus obtain that
C[r\sigma]\xRightarrow{p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}}\ldots\xRightarrow{p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}}C[r\theta]
.
Let us denote
\omega_{M}=(p,\theta:\,r\rightarrow\ell)
if
r\theta>\ell\theta
and
\omega_{M}=(p,\theta:\,r\leftarrow\ell)
otherwise. We thus have
C[r\theta]\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{M}}C[\ell\theta]
.
Similarly, if
q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}
are the positions of
x
in
\ell
other than
q_{x}
, we obtain that
C[\ell\theta]\xLeftarrow{p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}\ldots\xLeftarrow{p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}v
. Denoting
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}=(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\omega_{M}(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1})
, we obtain
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
. As
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}_{0}^{\prime}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
.
Additionally, by construction,
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
is syntactic from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\{r\rightarrow\ell\}
.

We now order the labels of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
w.r.t.
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
. Let us denote
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
. As
q_{x}
exists, if
m>0
then it implies that
q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}
are all different from
\varepsilon
. Thus, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, for all
\omega_{1}\in p\cdot q_{i}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
.
By definition, we know that
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and so
x\sigma>x\theta
, meaning that
r\sigma>r\theta
. Therefore, if
r\theta>\ell\theta
then we directly obtain
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})=(p,\ell\theta,r\theta)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)=\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Else we have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})=(p,r\theta,\ell\theta)
, which also implies implies
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
as
r\sigma>r\theta
.

Finally, let us look
\omega_{1}\in p\cdot p_{i}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}
for some
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. If
p_{i}\neq\varepsilon
or
q^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon
then not only we have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
but we also have that it holds for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
since
p_{i}\neq\varepsilon
implies for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
p_{j}\neq\varepsilon
. Thus, we now focus on
n=1
,
p_{1}=\varepsilon
and
q^{\prime}=\varepsilon
: It implies that
r=x
and
r\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. Thus,
r\sigma>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and so
\mathsf{rf}((p,\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))=(p,r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
.
We therefore conclude that for all
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
, there exist
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:

when
r\theta>\ell\theta

\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
t
v
r\leftarrow\ell
p,\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u=C[r\sigma]
C[r\theta]
C[\ell\theta]
v
(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})
r\rightarrow\ell
p,\theta
(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})

when
\ell\theta>r\theta

\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
t
v
r\leftarrow\ell
p,\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u=C[r\sigma]
C[r\theta]
C[\ell\theta]
v
(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})
r\leftarrow\ell
p,\theta
(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})

We already proved that, denoting
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\cup\{r\rightarrow\ell\})
, we have that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
is a syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Since
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
is from
\mathcal{R}
, it is naturally also from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Note that by
Definition

12
, we know that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}
and
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
. Thus, we can apply
Corollary

2
to deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
\mathcal{R}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
which
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into, and
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
. This conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 30
.

Assume that
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes. Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some ground rewrite steps. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
with
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
and
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms that is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p^{\prime}=p\cdot q
, and
q\not\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(r)
, and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

Proof.

By definition, we have there exist a term context
C[\_]
, two positions
q_{x},q^{\prime}
and a variable
x
such that
u=C[\ell\sigma]
,
C[\_]_{|p}=\_
,
t=C[r\sigma]
,
q=q_{x}\cdot q^{\prime}
,
\ell_{|q_{x}}=x\in\mathcal{X}
,
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and
C[r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]=v
.
Since
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=_{E_{\downarrow}}x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}
, there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. Hence, we can define
\mathsf{tr}_{M}=\mathsf{tr}_{L}(\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
to obtain
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}}r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=v_{|p^{\prime}}
. From
Lemma

15
, we deduce that
v_{|p^{\prime}}\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}
.

Let
\theta
be the substitution such that
y\theta=y\sigma
for all
y\in\mathit{vars}(r)\setminus\{x\}
and
x\theta=x\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q^{\prime}}
.
If
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
are the positions of
x
in
\ell
, thus obtain that
C[\ell\sigma]\xRightarrow{p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}}\ldots\xRightarrow{p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}}C[\ell\theta]
. As
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
, we deduce that
r\theta=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\theta
.
Denoting
\omega_{M}=(p,\theta:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
, we thus have
C[\ell\theta]\xRightarrow{\omega_{M}}C[r\theta]
.

Similarly, if
q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}
are the positions of
x
in
r
other than
q_{x}
, we obtain that
C[r\theta]\xLeftarrow{p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}\ldots\xLeftarrow{p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}v
. Denoting
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{1}=(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\omega_{M}(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1})
, we obtain
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
. As
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
.
Additionally, by construction,
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
is syntactic from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
.

We now order the labels of
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
w.r.t.
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
. Let us denote
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
. Since
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
and
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes, we know that
\ell
and
r
are not variables. Hence
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n},q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}
are not
\varepsilon
. Therefore, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, for all
\omega_{1}\in p\cdot q_{i}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
, and for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, for all
\omega_{1}\in p\cdot p_{i}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Recall that
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, meaning that
x\sigma>x\theta
and so
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma>r\theta=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell\theta
. We conclude that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
and so, combining all previous statements, we obtain
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
t
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u=C[\ell\sigma]
C[\ell\theta]
C[r\theta]
v
(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\theta
(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})

∎

Lemma 31
.

Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Let
u\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some ground rewrite steps. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
with
(\ell\rightarrow r),(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms that is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p^{\prime}=p\cdot q
, and
q\not\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(r)
, and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

Proof.

By definition, we have there exist a term context
C[\_]
, two positions
q_{x},q^{\prime}
and a variable
x
such that
u=C[\ell\sigma]
,
C[\_]_{|p}=\_
,
t=C[r\sigma]
,
q=q_{x}\cdot q^{\prime}
,
\ell_{|q_{x}}=x\in\mathcal{X}
,
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and
C[r\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q}]=v
.
Since
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=_{E_{\downarrow}}x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}
, there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. Hence, we can define
\mathsf{tr}_{M}=\mathsf{tr}_{L}(\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
to obtain
x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}}r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=v_{|p^{\prime}}
. From
Lemma

15
, we deduce that
v_{|p^{\prime}}\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}x\sigma_{|q^{\prime}}
.

Let
\theta
be the substitution such that
y\theta=y\sigma
for all
y\in\mathit{vars}(r)\setminus\{x\}
and
x\theta=x\sigma[r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]_{q^{\prime}}
.
If
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
are the positions of
x
in
\ell
, thus obtain that
C[\ell\sigma]\xRightarrow{p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}}\ldots\xRightarrow{p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}}C[\ell\theta]
.

Let us denote
\omega_{M}=(p,\theta:\,\ell\leftarrow r)
if
r\theta>\ell\theta
and
\omega_{M}=(p,\theta:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
otherwise. We thus have
C[\ell\theta]\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{M}}C[r\theta]
.
Similarly, if
q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}
are the positions of
x
in
r
other than
q_{x}
, we obtain that
C[r\theta]\xLeftarrow{p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}\ldots\xLeftarrow{p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}}v
. Denoting
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}=(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\omega_{M}(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1})
, we obtain
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
. As
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we obtain by construction of
\mathsf{tr}_{0}^{\prime}
that
\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}}v
.
Additionally, by construction,
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
is syntactic from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\{r\rightarrow\ell\}
.

We now order the labels of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
w.r.t.
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
. Let us denote
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
. As
q_{x}
exists, if
m>0
then it implies that
q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}
are all different from
\varepsilon
. Thus, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, for all
\omega_{1}\in p\cdot q_{i}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}^{-1}
,
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
.
By definition, we know that
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and so
x\sigma>x\theta
, meaning that
r\sigma>r\theta
. Therefore, if
r\theta>\ell\theta
then we directly obtain
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})=(p,\ell\theta,r\theta)\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}(p,r\sigma,\ell\sigma)=\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
. Else we have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})=(p,r\theta,\ell\theta)
, which also implies implies
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{M})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
as
r\sigma>r\theta
.

Finally, let us look
\omega_{1}\in p\cdot p_{i}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M}
for some
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. If
p_{i}\neq\varepsilon
or
q^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon
then not only we have
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega)
but we also have that it holds for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
since
p_{i}\neq\varepsilon
implies for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
p_{j}\neq\varepsilon
. Thus, we now focus on
n=1
,
p_{1}=\varepsilon
and
q^{\prime}=\varepsilon
: It implies that
\ell=x
and
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. However,
\ell
is a subterm of
r
, meaning that
\ell\sigma
is a subterm of
r\sigma
and so
\ell\sigma\not>r\sigma
(as
>
is a reduction ordering) which is a contradiction with
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
. Hence, we cannot have
p_{1}=\varepsilon=q^{\prime}
.
We therefore conclude that for all
\omega_{1}\in\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
, there exist
\omega_{0}\in\mathsf{tr}_{0}
such that
\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\omega_{0})
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
when
\ell\theta>r\theta
u
t
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u=C[\ell\sigma]
C[\ell\theta]
C[r\theta]
v
(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\theta
(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
when
r\theta>\ell\theta
u
t
v
\ell\rightarrow r
p,\sigma
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u=C[\ell\sigma]
C[\ell\theta]
C[r\theta]
v
(p\cdot p_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot p_{n}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{M})
\ell\leftarrow r
p,\theta
(p\cdot q_{1}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})\ldots(p\cdot q_{m}\cdot q^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{-1}_{M})

We already proved that, denoting
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{R}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\cup\{r\rightarrow\ell\})
, we have that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
is a syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Since
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
is from
\mathcal{R}
, it is naturally also from
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. Note that by
Definition

12
, we know that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}
and
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
. Thus, we can apply
Corollary

2
to deduce that there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
\mathcal{R}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
, and
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
. This conclude the proof.
∎

-D
4
Combining the different transformations

We can regroup the different result from the two previous sections in the following lemma:

Lemma 32
.

Assume that
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes. Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Assume that
\mathcal{R}^{=}
is not trivial. Let
u\xLeftrightarrow{\omega}t\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some ground rewrite steps such that
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\sim r)
and
{\sim}\in\{\rightarrow,\leftarrow\}
. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=\omega({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
with
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
and
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a set of terms that is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p\leq p^{\prime}
and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
then there exists a ground syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

Proof.

As
p\leq p^{\prime}
, there exists
q
such that
p^{\prime}=p\cdot q
. We do a case analysis on the shape of
\omega
and on the value of
q
.

•

Case
q\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(r)
:

–

{\sim}={\rightarrow}
and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
:
In that case,
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
. Note that
\mathcal{R}^{=}
is not trivial. If
\ell\in\mathcal{X}
and
\ell\not\in\mathit{vars}(r)
then we would have that for all terms
t
,
t=_{\mathcal{R}^{=}}r
which is a contradiction of
\mathcal{R}^{=}
not being trivial. We conclude by applying
Lemma

28
.

–

{\sim}={\rightarrow}
and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
:
We conclude by applying
Lemma

27
.

–

{\sim}={\leftarrow}
and
(r\rightarrow\ell)\in\mathcal{R}
:
In that case,
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
. We conclude by applying
Lemma

26
.

•

Case
q\not\in\mathcal{P}os_{\setminus\mathcal{X}}(r)
:

–

{\sim}={\rightarrow}
and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
:
In that case,
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
. We conclude by applying
Lemma

31
.

–

{\sim}={\rightarrow}
and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}
:
We conclude by applying
Lemma

30
.

–

{\sim}={\leftarrow}
and
(r\rightarrow\ell)\in\mathcal{R}
:
In that case,
\ell\sigma>r\sigma
. We conclude by applying
Lemma

29
.∎

Lemma 33
.

Let
\omega=(E,p,\sigma:\,\ell\sim r)
with
{\sim}\in\{\rightarrow,\leftarrow\}
. Let
u\xLeftrightarrow{\omega}t\xrightarrow[E^{\prime},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}v
be some rewrite steps. Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=\omega(E,p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})
. Let
\mathcal{M}
a set of terms that is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
.

If
p\mathrel{||}p^{\prime}
then by defining the ground rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{1}=(E,p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\omega
we have

P1

u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P2

\mathcal{M}
is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, and

P3

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})

P4

if
{\sim}={\leftarrow}
and either
r\sigma>\ell\sigma
or
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
then
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}_{0},v)<\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}_{1},v)

Proof.

Let us define
E_{1}=E
,
E_{2}=\emptyset
when
{\sim}={\rightarrow}
and
E_{1}=\emptyset
,
E_{2}=E
otherwise. Since
p\mathrel{||}p^{\prime}
, there exist a term context
C[\__{1},\__{2}]
and four terms
u^{\prime},v^{\prime},t_{1},t_{2}
such that
C[\__{1},\__{2}]_{|p}=\__{1}
and
C[\__{1},\__{2}]_{|p^{\prime}}=\__{2}
,
t=C[t_{1},t_{2}]
,
r\sigma=_{E_{2}}t_{1}
,
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=_{E^{\prime}}t_{2}
,
u^{\prime}=_{E_{1}}\ell\sigma
,
v^{\prime}=r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
,
u=C[u^{\prime},t_{2}]
and
v=C[t_{1},v^{\prime}]
. Defining
t^{\prime}=C[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]
, we deduce that
u\xrightarrow[E^{\prime},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}t^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega}v
, which gives us
Item

P1
.
Notice that as
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}v
, we directly obtain by construction that
\mathcal{M}
rewrites into
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
, i.e.
Item

P2
.
Notice that
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})=\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
, hence
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})
, i.e.
Item

P3
.
Finally, when
{\sim}={\leftarrow}
, since
>
is a
{E_{\downarrow}}
-compatible reduction ordering, we know from
u\xleftarrow[p,\sigma,E]{\ell\ \leftarrow\ r}t
that
t\geq u
, the inequality being strict when
r\sigma>\ell\sigma
. Moreover, as
u\xrightarrow[E^{\prime},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}t^{\prime}
, we deduce that
u\geq t^{\prime}
, the inequality being strict when
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
. Therefore, if
r\sigma>\ell\sigma
or
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}>r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
then we have
t>t^{\prime}
which concludes the proof of
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}_{0},v)<\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}_{1},v)
and thus
Item

P4
.

We can represent graphically the transformation as follows:
\xrightarrow{\mathit{transformation}}
u
t
v
\ell\leftarrow r
p,\sigma,E
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
E^{\prime},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
u
t^{\prime}
v
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
E^{\prime},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
\ell\leftarrow r
p,\sigma,E

∎

Lemma

33
allows us to consider an equivalence relation between rewrite traces, that allows to
swap
parallel positions.

Definition 13
.

Let
\approx_{\mathrel{||}}
the smallest equivalence relation on rewrite traces such that:

•

(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\approx_{\mathrel{||}}(p,\sigma:\,\ell\leftarrow r)
when
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}

•

(E_{1},p_{1},\sigma_{1}:\,\ell_{1}\sim_{1}r_{1})(E_{2},p_{2},\sigma_{2}:\,\ell_{2}\sim_{2}r_{2})\approx_{\mathrel{||}}(E_{2},p_{2},\sigma_{2}:\,\ell_{2}\sim_{2}r_{2})(E_{1},p_{1},\sigma_{1}:\,\ell_{1}\sim_{1}r_{1})
when
p_{1}\mathrel{||}p_{2}

•

\mathsf{tr}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
implies
\mathsf{tr}_{1}\cdot\mathsf{tr}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{2}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{1}\cdot\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{tr}_{2}

Lemma 34
.

Let
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
. Let
\mathcal{M}
a set of terms that is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
. For all rewrite traces
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
, if
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}
then
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
, and
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr})\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

A direct application
Lemma

33
on the definition of the equivalence relation
\approx_{\mathrel{||}}
.
∎

-D
5
Shape of minimal rewrite traces

Lemma 35
.

Assume that
{E_{\downarrow}}
has finite equivalence classes. Let
\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
be sets of rewrite rules such that
\mathcal{R}
is saturated w.r.t
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Assume that
\mathcal{R}^{=}
is not trivial. Let
u,v
two ground terms and
\mathsf{tr}
is a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a finite set of terms that is greater than
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
.

If
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
is minimal by
\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}
then there exist a term
t
and three ground syntactic rewrite traces
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime},\mathsf{tr}_{L},\mathsf{tr}_{R}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that:

•

\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}=\mathsf{tr}_{L}\mathsf{tr}_{R}
and
u\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}t\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}v

•

for all
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{L}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{L}
, if
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{L}=\mathsf{tr}_{1}(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\mathsf{tr}_{E}(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\mathsf{tr}_{2}
and
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
then
p\not\leq p^{\prime}

•

for all
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{R}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{R}
, if
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{R}=\mathsf{tr}_{1}(p,\sigma:\,r\leftarrow\ell)\mathsf{tr}_{E}(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,r^{\prime}\leftarrow\ell^{\prime})\mathsf{tr}_{2}
and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
then
p^{\prime}\not\leq p

Proof.

To prove the first point, we take the syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{tr}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
is minimal by
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(\cdot)
, i.e. for all
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
,
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime},v)\not<\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}^{\prime},v)
. Let us look at the shape of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
.

If
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}=\mathsf{tr}_{1}(p,\sigma:\,r\leftarrow\ell)(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\mathsf{tr}_{2}
then by
Lemma

33
, we know that
\ell\sigma\leq r\sigma
and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
, i.e.
\ell\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}r\sigma
and
\ell^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}=_{E_{\downarrow}}r^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
and so both
\ell\rightarrow r
and
\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}
are in
{E_{\downarrow}}
. Let us take the largest prefix
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
such that there exist two syntactic rewrite traces
\mathsf{tr}_{L},\mathsf{tr}_{R}
(possibly empty) and a two terms
t,s
such that
u\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}t\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}s
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{L}\mathsf{tr}_{R}
and
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime},s)=\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}_{L}\mathsf{tr}_{R},s)
.
If
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime}=\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
then the result holds. Otherwise,
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}=\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime}\omega\mathsf{tr}_{W}
for some rewrite labels
\omega
and a rewrite trace (possibly empty)
\mathsf{tr}_{W}
. By maximality of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime}
, we deduce that
\mathsf{tr}_{R}\neq\varepsilon
and
\omega=(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)
.
As
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime},s)=\mathsf{rf}_{2}(u,\mathsf{tr}_{L}\mathsf{tr}_{R},s)
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
is minimal by
\mathsf{rf}_{2}(\cdot)
, we already showed that
\ell\rightarrow r
must be in
{E_{\downarrow}}
. This is a contradiction with the maximality of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime\prime}
as, in such a case,
(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\approx_{\mathrel{||}}(p,\sigma:\,\ell\leftarrow r)
. This concludes the proof of the first item of the lemma.

We now show the second and third properties of the lemma. In particular, we show the following sub-property: if
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=\mathsf{tr}_{1}(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\mathsf{tr}_{E}(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\mathsf{tr}_{2}
and
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
and
p\leq p^{\prime}
and
t_{0}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{0}}t_{5}
smaller than
\mathcal{M}
then there exists a syntactic rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}_{T}
such that:

•

t_{0}\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{T}}t_{5}
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
,

•

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{T})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
.

Let
\mathsf{tr}_{E}
be the smallest rewrite trace (in term of
|\mathsf{tr}_{E}|
) such that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{0}
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}=\mathsf{tr}_{1}(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\mathsf{tr}_{E}(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\mathsf{tr}_{2}
and
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
and
p\leq p^{\prime}
. There exist
t_{1},\ldots,t_{4}
such that:

t_{0}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}t_{1}\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}t_{2}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{E}}t_{3}\xrightarrow[p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}t_{4}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{2}}t_{5}

If
(p^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime})\in\mathsf{tr}_{E}
and
(\ell^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
then by minimality of
\mathsf{tr}_{E}
, we know that
p\not\leq p^{\prime\prime}
and
p^{\prime\prime}\not\leq p^{\prime}
.

Let us show that for all
\omega^{\prime\prime}=(p^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime})\in\mathsf{tr}_{E}
,
p^{\prime\prime}\not\approx_{\mathrel{||}}p^{\prime}
. By contradiction, w.l.o.g., assume that
\mathsf{tr}_{E}=\mathsf{tr}_{A}\omega^{\prime\prime}\mathsf{tr}_{B}
, and
p^{\prime\prime}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}p^{\prime}
and for all
\omega_{2}\in\mathsf{tr}_{B}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega_{2})\not\approx_{\mathrel{||}}p^{\prime}
. We deduce that for all
\omega_{2}\in\mathsf{tr}_{B}
,
\mathit{pos}(\omega_{2})>p^{\prime}
. Moreover, as
p^{\prime\prime}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}p^{\prime}
, we deduce that
\mathit{pos}(\omega_{2})\approx_{\mathrel{||}}p^{\prime\prime}
. This would imply that
\mathsf{tr}_{0}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{1}(p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\mathsf{tr}_{A}\mathsf{tr}_{B}(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\omega^{\prime\prime}\mathsf{tr}_{2}
, which is a contradiction with the minimality of
\mathsf{tr}_{E}
.

As we shown that
\omega^{\prime\prime}=(p^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime})\in\mathsf{tr}_{E}
,
p^{\prime\prime}\not\approx_{\mathrel{||}}p^{\prime}
and
p^{\prime\prime}\not\leq p^{\prime}
, we deduce that
p^{\prime}<p^{\prime\prime}
and so
p<p^{\prime\prime}
. Once again by minimality of
\mathsf{tr}_{E}
, we deduce that
(\ell^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime})\not\in\mathcal{R}
, i.e.
(\ell^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime\prime})\in{E_{\downarrow}}
. It implies that
t_{1}\xrightarrow[p,\sigma]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}t_{2}\xrightarrow[{E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}]{\ell^{\prime}\ \rightarrow\ r^{\prime}}t_{4}
. Hence by
Lemma

32
, we deduce that there exist a syntactic trace
\mathsf{tr}_{S}
from
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup\mathcal{R}
such that:

•

t_{1}\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{S}}t_{4}
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
,

•

\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{S})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}((p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))
.

As
\mathsf{rf}((p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)({E_{\downarrow}},p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))=\mathsf{rf}((p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}((p,\sigma:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\mathsf{tr}_{E}(p^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime}))
, we obtain
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1}\mathsf{tr}_{S}\mathsf{tr}_{2})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0})
. We conclude by taking
\mathsf{tr}_{T}=\mathsf{tr}_{1}\mathsf{tr}_{S}\mathsf{tr}_{2}
.

The second property of the lemma directly holds by applying the sub-property on
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=\mathsf{tr}_{L}
leading to
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1}\mathsf{tr}_{S}\mathsf{tr}_{2})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0})\backsimeq_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})
and so
\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr}_{1}\mathsf{tr}_{S}\mathsf{tr}_{2}\mathsf{tr}_{R})\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{rf}(\mathsf{tr})
yielding a contradiction with the minimality of
\mathsf{tr}
by
\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}
. The third property of the lemma holds by applying the sub-property on
\mathsf{tr}_{0}=\mathsf{tr}_{R}^{-1}
which will also lead to a contradiction with the minimality of
\mathsf{tr}
by
\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}
.
∎

Recall we placed ourselves within the hypotheses of
Theorem

5
. Hence
generate_rw_th(
E^{\prime},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}
)
terminates and returns
\mathcal{R}
such that for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
,
\mathit{vars}(r)\subseteq\mathit{vars}(\ell)
. We also denote
E=E^{\prime}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}^{=}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
and
T=(>,\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}})
.

Lemma 36
.

For all ground terms
u,v
, if
u=_{E}v
then there exist a ground term
w
and two rewrite traces
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
such that:

•

rules in
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
are from
\mathcal{R}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
, and

•

u\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}w\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}v
, and

•

\mathsf{tr}_{L},\mathsf{tr}_{R}
are both ordered by decreasing position.

Proof.

As
u,v
are ground and
u=_{E}v
, we deduce that
u=t_{0}\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}t_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}t_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}\ldots\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}t_{n}=v
where
\mathcal{R}
is the rewrite system composed of either rules from
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
or rules
\ell\rightarrow r
where either
(\ell=r)\in{E_{\downarrow}}\cup E^{\prime}
or
(r=\ell)\in{E_{\downarrow}}\cup E^{\prime}
. Note that some of the
t_{i}
s could potentially contain variables, i.e. not be ground. As rewriting is stable by application of substitutions, we can take
\sigma
with
\mathit{dom}(\sigma)=\mathit{vars}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})
and
\mathit{img}(\sigma)\subseteq\mathcal{N}
. Note that the image of
\sigma
does not really matter other than being ground. As
u,v
are ground, we thus obtain that
u=t_{0}=t_{0}\sigma\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}t_{1}\sigma\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}t_{2}\sigma\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}\ldots\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}}t_{n}\sigma=t_{n}=v
. Since
>
is
{E_{\downarrow}}
-total, we can thus order each pair
(t_{i}\sigma,t_{i+1}\sigma)
with
>
, i.e. either
t_{i}\sigma>t_{i+1}\sigma
or
t_{i}\sigma<t_{i+1}\sigma
or
t_{i}\sigma=_{E_{\downarrow}}t_{i+1}\sigma
. Moreover, as
>
is
{E_{\downarrow}}
-compatible, we deduce the existence of
n+1
ground syntactic rewrite labels
\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{n+1}
such that
u=t_{0}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{1}}t_{1}\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{2}}\ldots\xLeftrightarrow{\omega_{n}}t_{n}=v
. Denoting
\mathsf{tr}=\omega_{1}\ldots\omega_{n}
and
\mathcal{M}=\{\!\!\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}\!\!\}
, we deduce that
\mathsf{tr}
is a ground syntactic trace from
\mathcal{R}
and
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}}v
is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
.

Let us denote by
\mathcal{R}_{0},\mathcal{R}_{1},\ldots
the successive values of
\mathcal{R}
in
Algorithm

1
. Similarly, we denote by
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots
the successive values of
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
in
Algorithm

1
. In particular, from
Algorithm

1
, we have
\mathcal{R}_{0}={\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}\cup\{\ell\rightarrow r,r\rightarrow\ell\mid(\ell=r)\in E^{\prime}\}
. By denoting
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{0}=\mathcal{R}_{0}
, we obtain that for all
i\geq 1
,
\mathcal{R}_{i}=

normalise(
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i-1},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}
)
, that is
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i-1}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{i}
. Hence the rewrite trace
\mathsf{tr}
is from
\mathcal{R}_{0}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. By
Lemma

25
, we know that for all
i\geq 0
,
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\cup\bigcup_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{j}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
.

We prove by induction that for all
i\geq 1
, there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{i}
a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}_{i}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{i}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
. In the base case
i=1
, on
Algorithm

1
of
Algorithm

1
, we have
\mathcal{R}_{1}=

normalise(
\mathcal{R}_{0},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}
)
, i.e.
\mathcal{R}_{0}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{1}
. By
Corollary

2
, we deduce that there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
.
In the inductive step
i>1
, by inductive hypothesis, we know that there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{i-1}
a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{i-1}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
.
By definition,
\mathcal{R}_{i}=

normalise(
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i-1},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}
)
, i.e.
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i-1}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{i}
. By
Lemma

24
,
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i-1}\cup\bigcup_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{j}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{i}\cup\bigcup_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{j}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{i}
.
Note that
\mathsf{tr}_{i-1}
being a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}
implies that
\mathsf{tr}_{i-1}
is also from
\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cup\bigcup_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{j}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
. As
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{0}
contains
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
, we can apply
Corollary

to deduce that there exists
\mathsf{tr}_{i}
a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}_{i}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{i}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
.

We now complete the proof our the lemma.
As the algorithm terminates, there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathcal{R}_{N}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{N}\rightsquigarrow_{{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}}}^{*}\mathcal{R}_{N}
, i.e. it reached a fix-point. From
Algorithms

,
1
,
1
and
1
of
Algorithm

, we deduce that
\mathcal{R}_{N}
is saturated w.r.t.
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}
. Moreover, we already proved that for all

,
\ell=_{E}r
and by hypothesis,

is not trivial. It implies that
\mathcal{R}_{N}^{=}
is also not trivial. Recall that
\mathcal{M}
is finite and ground. Since

is a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}_{N}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{i}}v
smaller than
\mathcal{M}
, we can take
\mathsf{tr}_{min}
to be a ground syntactic rewrite trace from
\mathcal{R}_{N}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}
such that
u\xLeftrightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{i}}v
which is smaller than
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{min}
is minimal by
\prec_{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{M})}
.

By
Lemma

35
, we deduce that there exist a term
w
and three ground syntactic well-ordered rewrite traces
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{min}
,
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
,
\mathsf{tr}_{R}
from

such that:

1.

\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{min}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{min}
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{min}=\mathsf{tr}_{L}\mathsf{tr}_{R}
and
u\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}w\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}v

2.

for all

, if
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{L}=\mathsf{tr}_{A}(p,\alpha:\,\ell\rightarrow r)\mathsf{tr}_{E}(p^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}:\,\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\mathsf{tr}_{B}
and
(\ell^{\prime}\rightarrow r^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}
then
p\not\leq p^{\prime}

3.

for all
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{R}\approx_{\mathrel{||}}\mathsf{tr}_{R}
, if
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{R}=\mathsf{tr}_{A}(p,\alpha:\,r\leftarrow\ell)\mathsf{tr}_{E}(p^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}:\,r^{\prime}\leftarrow\ell^{\prime})\mathsf{tr}_{B}
and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
then
p^{\prime}\not\leq p

This allows us to conclude.

See

Proof.

We first show that
T=(>,\mathcal{R},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}})
is a rewrite theory. This is a simple matter as
Items

S1
and
S2
are given as assumptions. Finally, as we assumed that for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
, we have
\mathit{vars}(r)\subseteq\mathit{vars}(\ell)
, and since
Algorithm

1
of
Algorithm

1
ensures that
\mathcal{R}
contains
f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\rightarrow f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
with arity
n
,
Item

S3
is guaranteed.

Let us now prove that
T
mimics
E
. Amongst the three properties required to show that
T
mimics
E
, only the last one, i.e.
Item

M3
, is difficult.
Item

M1
is directly obtained, since
{E_{\downarrow}}={E_{\mathcal{A}}}
and
{E_{\downarrow}}\cup{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}^{=}\cup E^{\prime}=E
. The proof of
Item

M2
is done a simple induction on the number of loop the algorithm went through in
Algorithm

1
. Indeed, all rules
\ell\rightarrow r
in the initial value
\mathcal{R}
on
Algorithm

1
of
Algorithm

1
satisfy
\ell=_{E}r
. By applying
Lemma

3
and noticing that the normalisation rules
NormL
and
NormR
preserve this invariant, since
{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}}^{=}\cup{E_{\downarrow}}\subseteq E
, we obtain that
\mathcal{R}
satisfies
Item

M2
.

We now focus on the proof of
Item

M3
. Consider
f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})=_{E}t
and
\mathcal{M}=\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{n},t\}
. If
\mathrm{nf}_{T,E}(\mathcal{M})
holds then by
Definition

3
we know that
u\theta=_{E_{\downarrow}}{\min_{E,>}}(u\theta)
for all
u\in\mathcal{M}
where
\theta
is an injective substitution closing
f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})
and
t
. Hence, by
Lemma

36
, we have

f(t_{1}\theta,\ldots,t_{n}\theta)\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{L}}w\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}t\theta

However,
w\xLeftarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{R}}t\theta
implies that
w<t\theta
or
w=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\theta
. Since
t\theta=_{E_{\downarrow}}{\min_{E,>}}(t\theta)
, we deduce that
w=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\theta
.

As
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
is ordered by decreasing position, we know that there can only be one rule applied at root position, i.e.
\varepsilon
, that can strictly decrease the terms. Moreover, as
t_{i}\theta=_{E_{\downarrow}}{\min_{E,>}}(t_{i}\theta)
for all
i
, we also know that any rule application in
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
before the one at root position cannot strictly decrease the terms (otherwise
{\min_{E,>}}(t_{i}\theta)
would not be minimal). In other words, either

(a)

f(t_{1}\theta,\ldots,t_{n}\theta)=_{E_{\downarrow}}w
; or

(b)

we have:

f(t_{1}\theta,\ldots,t_{n}\theta)\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{1}}s\xrightarrow[\varepsilon,\alpha]{\ell\ \rightarrow\ r}w^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{tr}_{2}}w

where all rules in
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{2}
are in
{E_{\downarrow}}
, and
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
with
\ell\alpha>r\alpha
. Additionally, we also know that the positions in
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
are different from
\varepsilon
, as
\mathsf{tr}_{L}
is ordered by decreasing position. Case (a) implies
f(t_{1}\theta,\ldots,t_{n}\theta)=_{E_{\downarrow}}t\theta
and so
f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})=_{E_{\downarrow}}t
. We conclude by taking the rule
f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\rightarrow f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
and
\sigma=\{x_{1}\mapsto t_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\mapsto t_{n}\}
. Case (b) implies that
t_{i}\theta=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell_{|i}\alpha
and so
t_{i}=_{E_{\downarrow}}\ell_{|i}\alpha\theta^{-1}
for all
i
, and
r\alpha\theta^{-1}=_{E_{\downarrow}}t
. We thus conclude with the rule
\ell\rightarrow r
and
\sigma=\alpha\theta^{-1}
.

∎

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{0},\mathcal{R}_{1},\ldots
the successive values of
\mathcal{R}
in

. We prove by induction on

that
(>,\mathcal{R}_{n},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}},{E_{\mathcal{A}}})
mimics

. The base case
n=0
is given by hypothesis. In the inductive step, we know that
(>,\mathcal{R}_{n-1},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}},{E_{\mathcal{A}}})
mimics

. The only non-trivial property to prove in order to show that
(>,\mathcal{R}_{n},{\mathcal{R}_{\downarrow}},{E_{\downarrow}},{E_{\mathcal{A}}})
mimics

is

M3
.
Take
f(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})=_{E}t
and
\mathrm{nf}_{T,E}(\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{n},t\})
. We know that there exist
\sigma
and
f(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})\rightarrow s
in

such that
t=_{E_{\mathcal{A}}}s\sigma
and for all

t_{i}=_{E_{\mathcal{A}}}s_{i}\sigma
. If

Else, from

, there exist a substitution
\alpha
and a rule
f(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n})\rightarrow\ell
in

such that
\ell\alpha=_{{E_{\mathcal{A}}}}s

. Therefore, it implies

and for all

,

. This allows us to conclude.

Order
E
-compatible

See
6

Proof.

We define
>^{\prime}_{2}
as the following relation:
u>^{\prime}_{2}v
if and only if

•

either
u>v

•

or
u\equiv v
and there exist
s,t
ground terms and a context
C[\_]
such that
u=_{E}C[s]
,
v=_{E}C[t]
and
s>_{1}t

Finally, we define the
>_{2}
as being the transitive closure of
>^{\prime}_{2}
.

Let us prove that
>_{2}
is a
E
-strong reduction order compatible with
\mathcal{R}
.

Closed by application of contexts and substitutions:

Assume
u>^{\prime}_{2}v
and let
D[\_]
be a context and
\sigma
be a substitution. If
u>v
then as
>
is a reduction order, we have that
D[u\sigma]>D[v\sigma]
and so
D[u\sigma]>^{\prime}_{2}D[v\sigma]
. Otherwise,
u\equiv v
and there exists
s,t
ground terms and a context
C[\_]
such that
u=_{E}C[s]
,
v=_{E}C[t]
and
s>_{1}t
. Since
s
and
t
are ground, there exists a context
D^{\prime}[\_]=D[C\sigma[\_]]
such that
D[u\sigma]=_{E}D^{\prime}[s]
and
D[v\sigma]=_{E}D^{\prime}[s]
. Since
\equiv
is close by application of contexts and substitutions, we deduce that
D[u\sigma]\equiv D[v\sigma]
. This allows us to deduce that
D[u\sigma]>^{\prime}_{2}D[v\sigma]
meaning that
>^{\prime}_{2}
is closed by application of contexts and substitutions.
Since
>_{2}
is the transitive closure of
>^{\prime}_{2}
, we conclude that
>_{2}
is closed by application of contexts and substitutions.

Transitive:

By definition of
>_{2}
.

Asymmetric and irreflexive:

if
a>_{2}b
and
b>_{2}a
then there exists
a>^{\prime}_{2}u_{1}>^{\prime}_{2}\ldots>^{\prime}_{2}u_{n}>^{\prime}_{2}b>^{\prime}_{2}v_{1}>^{\prime}_{2}\ldots>^{\prime}_{2}v_{m}>^{\prime}_{2}a
. Since
>
is
\equiv
-compatible, we deduce from definition of
>^{\prime}_{2}
that
a>^{\prime}_{2}u_{1}>^{\prime}_{2}\ldots>^{\prime}_{2}u_{n}>^{\prime}_{2}b
implies that either
a>b
or
a\equiv b
(and
a\equiv u_{1}\equiv\ldots u_{n}\equiv b
). Similarly,
b>^{\prime}_{2}v_{1}>^{\prime}_{2}\ldots>^{\prime}_{2}v_{m}>^{\prime}_{2}a
implies that either
b>a
or
a\equiv b
. If
a>b
then we cannot have
b>a
since
>
is a strict order and we also cannot have
a\equiv b
since
\equiv
-compatibility
would imply that
a>a
which is prevented by
>
being a strict order. With the same reasoning, we can show that
b>a
. It entails that
a\equiv b
. Note that by definition of
>^{\prime}_{2}
and since
>_{1}
is closed by application of contexts and
E
-compatible, we deduce that
a>_{1}u_{1}>_{1}\ldots u_{n}>_{1}b>_{1}v_{1}>_{1}\ldots>_{1}v_{m}>_{1}a
. This is in contradiction with
>_{1}
being a strict order.

Well founded:

We only need to show that the relation
>^{\prime}_{2}
is well founded since
>_{2}
is the transitive closure of
>^{\prime}_{2}
. Consider an infinite sequence
u_{1}>^{\prime}_{2}u_{2}>^{\prime}_{2}u_{3}>^{\prime}_{2}\ldots
. As
>
is well founded and by
\equiv
-compatibility we deduce that we cannot have an infinite sub-sequence
u_{i}>u_{i+1}>u_{i+2}>\ldots
and we cannot have infinite alternation of
\equiv
and
>
. This allows us to deduce that there exists an infinite sub-sequence
u_{j}>_{1}u_{j+1}>_{1}u_{j+2}>_{1}\ldots
(recall that
>_{1}
is closed by application of contexts). This contradicts the fact that
>_{1}
is well-founded.

E
-compatible:

Take
s=_{E}u>^{\prime}_{2}v=_{E}t
. By definition,
s=_{E}u
implies
s\equiv u
, and
v=_{E}t
implies
v\equiv t
. If
u>v
then we deduce that
s>t
(by
\equiv
-compatibility) and so
s>^{\prime}_{2}t
. Otherwise
u\equiv v
and there exist
a,b
ground terms and a context
C[\_]
such that
u=_{E}C[a]
,
v=_{E}C[b]
and
a>_{1}b
. Hence
s=_{E}C[a]
and
t=_{E}C[b]
and
a>_{1}b
. Therefore
s>^{\prime}_{2}t
. To complete the proof, we can notice that if
s=_{E}u>_{2}v=_{E}t
then
s=_{E}u>^{\prime}_{2}a_{1}>^{\prime}_{2}\ldots>^{\prime}_{2}a_{n}>^{\prime}_{2}v=_{E}t
. As
s=_{E}u>^{\prime}_{2}a_{1}
implies
s>^{\prime}_{2}a_{1}
, and
a_{n}>^{\prime}_{2}v=_{E}t
implies
a_{n}>^{\prime}_{2}t
, we conclude that
s>^{\prime}_{2}a_{1}>^{\prime}_{2}\ldots>^{\prime}_{2}a_{n}>^{\prime}_{2}t
and so
s>_{2}t
.

E
-total:

Let
a,b
be ground terms. By
\equiv
-total property, we know that either
a>b
or
b>a
or
a\equiv b
. In the two first case, we obtain
a>_{2}b
and
b>_{2}a
respectively. In the last case, as
a
and
b
are ground, we know that either
a=_{E}b
or
a>_{1}b
or
b>_{1}a
. If
a>_{1}b
(resp.
b>_{1}a
) then by taking the empty context
C[\_]=\_
, we obtain that
a=_{E}C[a]
and
b=_{E}C[b]
which leads to
a>^{\prime}_{2}b
(resp.
b>^{\prime}_{2}a
) and so
a>_{2}b
(resp.
b>_{2}a
).

Compatible with
\mathcal{R}
:

By hypothesis, for all
(\ell\rightarrow r)\in\mathcal{R}
, we have
\ell>r
and so
\ell>_{2}r
.

Stable by renaming:

Recall that due to
\equiv
-compatibility of
>
, we know that if
u\sim_{1}\ldots\sim_{i}s>t\sim_{i+1}\ldots\sim_{n}v
with
\sim_{i}\in\{{\equiv},{>}\}
for all
i
then
u>v
. Furthermore, by definition of
>_{2}
, if
u>_{2}v
and
u\not>v
then
u\equiv v
and
u>_{1}v
. Hence, we obtain that
u>_{2}v
implies that
u>v
or
u>_{1}v
.

Let us now look at the case where we order names: If
a>_{2}b
then
a>b
or
a>_{1}b
. By hypothesis,
a>b
implies
a>_{1}b
. Therefore
a>_{2}b
implies
a>_{1}b
. Assume now that
a>_{1}b
. By
\equiv
-totality, we know that either
a\equiv b
,
a>b
or
b>a
. The latter case is in contradiction with the fact that it would imply
b>_{1}a
. Hence, either
a>b
or
a\equiv b
. In both cases, as
a>_{1}b
, we deduce that
a>_{2}b
. This conclude the proof of
a>_{2}b
iff
a>_{1}b
.
This property allows us to prove that a renaming
\rho
preserves
>_{2}
iff
\rho
preserves
>_{1}
.

Let us now show that if
\rho
preserves
>_{2}
then
\rho
preserves
>
. Take
a,b\in\mathit{dom}(\rho)
. If
a>b
then
a>_{2}b
which implies
a\rho>_{2}b\rho
. By definition, either
a\rho>b\rho
or
a\rho\equiv b\rho
. In the latter case, since
\equiv
is stable by application of renaming, we deduce that
a\equiv b
. However, by
\equiv
-compatibility, it contradicts our hypothesis
a>b
. Thus
a\rho>b\rho
. Let us assume that
a\rho>b\rho
. We can in fact apply the same reasoning:
a\rho>b\rho
implies
a\rho>_{2}b\rho
and
a>_{2}b
. By definition, either
a>b
or
a\equiv b
. Once again the latter case is in contradiction with
a\rho>_{2}b\rho
by
\equiv
-compatibility and the stability of
\equiv
by application of renamings.

We can now complete the proof of stability by renaming: Consider a renaming
\rho
preserving
>_{2}
and two terms
u
and
v
such that
\mathit{names}(u,v)\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\rho)
. We already proved that
\rho
preserves both
>_{1}
and
>
. We first prove that
u>^{\prime}_{2}v
if and only if
u\rho>^{\prime}_{2}v\rho
. Assume that
u>^{\prime}_{2}v
. By definition we have:

•

either
u>v
: In such a case, as
>
is stable by renaming, we deduce that
u\rho>v\rho
and so
u\rho>_{2}v\rho
.

•

or
u\equiv v
and there exist ground terms
s,t
and a context
C[\_]
such that
u=_{E}C[s]
,
v=_{E}C[t]
and
s>_{1}t
: In such a case, as
\equiv
is stable by application of renamings,
u\rho\equiv v\rho
. Moreover, as
\rho
preserves
>_{1}
and
>_{1}
is stable by renaming, we deduce that
u\rho=_{E}C\rho[s\rho]
,
v\rho=_{E}C\rho[t\rho]
and
s\rho>_{1}t\rho
. Therefore
u\rho>_{2}v\rho
.

The other implication works in a similar fashion with a small difference when
u\rho>^{\prime}_{2}v\rho
due to
u\rho=_{E}C[s]
,
v\rho=_{E}C[t]
and
s>_{1}t
: Recall that
\mathit{names}(u,v)\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\rho)
. Hence
u=_{E}C\rho^{-1}[s\rho^{-1}]
and
v=_{E}C\rho^{-1}[t\rho^{-1}]
. Moreover,
\rho
preserves
>_{1}
implies
\rho^{-1}
preserves
>_{1}
, meaning that
s\rho^{-1}>_{1}t\rho^{-1}
. This allows us to conclude that
u>_{2}v
.
∎

Corollary 3
.

Consider
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}
the rewrite system
AC
-convergent for
\mathcal{AG}
, first proposed by Lankford
[
41
]
, defined below.

\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ \rightarrow\ }l r@{\ \rightarrow\ }l}x*1&x&{x^{-1}}^{-1}&x\\
1^{-1}&1&(x^{-1}*y)^{-1}&x*y^{-1}\\
x*x^{-1}&1&x*(x^{-1}*y)&y\\
x^{-1}*y^{-1}&(x*y)^{-1}&x^{-1}*(y^{-1}*z)&(x*y)^{-1}*z\\
(x*y)^{-1}*y&x^{-1}&(x*y)^{-1}*(y*z)&x^{-1}*z\\
\end{array}\right.

There exists a
E
-strong reduction order compatible with
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}
.

Proof.

For a term
u
, let us note
\#(u)
the number of function symbols in
u
.

We define
\equiv
the smallest equivalence relation such that
u\equiv v
implies
u=_{AC}v
or there exist
a,b
ground terms and a term context
C[\_]
such that
u=C[a]
and
C[b]=v
and
\#(a)=\#(b)
.

Let us now define
>^{\prime}
be the order defined as
u>^{\prime}v
if and only if either
u\equiv\circ\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}}\circ\equiv v
or else there exist
a,b
ground terms and a term context
C[\_]
such that
u\equiv C[a]
and
C[b]\equiv v
and
\#(a)>\#(b)
. Finally, the relation
>_{\mathcal{AG}}
is the transitive closure of
>^{\prime}
.

By definition of
AC
, we can easily notice that
u\equiv v
implies that
\#(u)=\#(v)
. Additionally, by definition of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}
, we also have that for
\ell\rightarrow r\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}
, for all substitutions
\sigma
,
\#(\ell\sigma)>\#(r\sigma)
. Hence
u\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}/AC}v
implies
\#(u)>\#(v)
. Thus, we obtain that
>^{\prime}
is well founded and so does
>_{\mathcal{AG}}
.
It can also easily be shown that
>_{\mathcal{AG}}
is closed by application of substitutions and contexts. Hence
>_{\mathcal{AG}}
is a reduction order.

Take
a,b
two ground terms. Assume that
a\equiv\circ\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}/AC}\circ\equiv b
or
b\equiv\circ\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}/AC}\circ\equiv a
, we directly have that
a>_{\mathcal{AG}}b
or
b>_{\mathcal{AG}}a
. Else, we naturally have either
\#(a)>\#(b)
or
\#(a)=\#(b)
or
\#(a)<\#(b)
. In the first case, we have
a>_{\mathcal{AG}}b
. In the second case,
a\equiv b
. In the third case,
b>_{\mathcal{AG}}a
. This prove
\equiv
-totality of
>_{\mathcal{AG}}
.

Finally, assume
s\equiv u>^{\prime}v\equiv t
. If
u>^{\prime}v
because
u\equiv\circ\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}/AC}\circ\equiv v
then we naturally have
s\equiv\circ\rightarrow_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}/AC}\circ\equiv t
and so
s>^{\prime}t
. Otherwise,
u\equiv C[a]
and
C[b]\equiv v
and
\#(a)>\#(b)
for some ground terms
a,b
and context
C[\_]
. Hence, we also naturally have
s\equiv C[a]
and
C[b]\equiv t
which allows us to conclude that
s>^{\prime}t
. As
>_{\mathcal{AG}}
is the transitive closure of
>^{\prime}
, it concludes the proof of
\equiv
-compatibility.

We conclude the existence of a
E
-strong reduction order compatible with
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{AG}}
by applying
Lemma

6
with the order
>_{\mathcal{AG}}
and the order in
[
48
]
that was shown to be a
AC
-strong reduction order (
Section

VII
).
∎
